                 MEMORANDUM FOR RECORD


PROBLEM:

      1. To return a photographic negative purported to be
ot "flying saucers" taken by a member of the USCG at Salem
Air Station, Massachusetts, on 16 July 1952.
FACTS AND DISCUSSION:
      2. On 15 December 1952, Mrs. Mead of the Public Intonna­
tion Division ot the USCG here in Washington telephoned Captain
Smith of AFOIN-2A2 and requested return of subject negative.
      3. Captain Smith contacted Captain Ruppelt o! ATIC on
16 December 1952 by telephone and requested he bring the negativ~
to HQ USAF on a sta.tf visit which he was to make on 17-18 December.
      4. Captain Ruppelt stated that ATIC was finished with
 analyzing the photograph and that he would bring it with him
for return to the USCG.
ACTION:
      ; . Subject negative returned by attached letter in com­
pliance with the request of the USCG.

COORDrnA.TION:
  AFOIN-2A2, Col. W. H. Smith, Ext. 71016
  AFOIN-2A, Col. W. A. Adams, Ext. 74072

                        MEMORANDUM FOR RECORD
PROBUX:

    1. To prepare a letter to the Department of State tor the Secretary
of Detonse.
FACTS AND DISCUSSION:
     2. In a letter dated 13 November 1952, the Department of State
transmitted to the Secretary of Defense, a copy of a letter trom Mr.
Jacques Petitjean Roget of Martinique, F. w. I. reporting the obserYation
of an unidentified flying object. 'lbe Department of State requested
in!ormation regarding our desire to obtain additional reports ot this
nature.
     3. The Office of the Secretary ot De.tense instructed the Air Force
to prepare & direct reply to the Department of State.
     4. 'lb• attached reply to the Dapartnlent of State expresses the
appreciation ot the Air Force tor their cooperation in this matter and
indicates our desire to receive additional reports which they mention
are &Tailable from this area.
     5. Subject matter is not considered suitable tor inclusion in the
Daily Staff Digest.
RECCIOO:NDATIONS:
     6. Approval, signature and forwarding of attached reply.

      24-0                                               6 October 1950

      SUBJECT:   UNCONVE1d'IONAL AIRCRAFT




      TO:        District Commander
                 5th District Office of Special Investigations
                 Wright-Patterson Air Force Base
                 Dayton, Ohio



           1. Transmitted under separate cover is approximately fifteen
      feet of moving picture film taken by Mr. Nicholas Mariana, of 2919
      First Avenue North, Great Falls, Montana, who is general manager
      of the Great Falls Baseball Club.
           2. Mr. Mariana made these pictures on 15 August 1950 between
      the hours of 11:25 and ll:30 A. M., at Great Falls, Montana. On
      interview, he stated that he first fighted the objects while standing
      in the gx·andstand of the ball park located at 26th Street and Eleventh
      Avenue North, Great Falls, Montana, and that he saw two bright disc
      shaped objects proceeding in a southerly direction at an alti tude
      of approximately ten thousand feet and at a distance of about three­
      quarters of a mile in a westerly direction, fr.:>m the observer•s view­
      point. Mr. Mariana then stated that he ran downstairs and outside
      to his car from which he obtained his camera and made the pictures
      shown in the film. From the time of first sighting to the
      completion of taking of the pictures, Mr. Mariana estimated con­
      awned approximately twenty seconds. At the time of his taking
      the pictures, a Miss Virginia Raunig, l.12 Second Avenue North,
      Great Falls, Montana, who is anployed as his secretary, was a
      witness.
           3. The water tower in instant pictures is located approximately
      seven hundred feet in a southwesterl y direction from the point at
      which Mr. Mariana stood when taking the pictures and the tower is
      approximately one hundred seventy- five feet in height.




                                                                 COPY

--- PAGE 20 ---
C OP Y
     24- 0                                                    6 October 1950
     SUBJECT:    UNCONVENTIONAL AIRCRAFT

          4. With reference to background information as regards Nicholas
     Mariana, he is a graduate of Montana State University, 1938, with a
     BA in journalism. He was born in Miles City, Montana, in 1912, went
     to Montana State lniversity in 1935 and has, since graduation trom
     that school, resided in Helena from 1940 to 1941 and from 1941 until
     the present at Great Falls. During the war, Mr. Mariana served in the
     Army Air Forces from June 1943 to October 1945, attaining the rank of
     Corporal and was editor ot a newspaper, The Northern Dispatch, at
     Great Falls Air Force Base, at Great Falls, Montana. Subject ha~ been
     married for a period o! ten years and has one chi1d, a daughter, three
     years of age• Presently Mr. Mariana is employed as general manager of
     the Great Falls Baseball Club, Great Falls, Montant, and also as a
     radio sports commentator and radio announcer of sporting events. He
     enjoys an excellent reputation in the local community and is regarded
     as a reliable, trustworthy and honest individual.
          5. Prior to concluding the interview with Mr. Mariana, he
     was advised that the film was being forwarded to the Air Materiel
     Command because of that agency's interest in anything which would
     contribute to its fund of technical intelligence data.



     l. Incl.                                       JOHN P. BRYNILDSEN
         Swmnary of Information                     Captain, USAF
         per provisions of Air                      District Commander

   24-0                                                      6 October 1950

   MEMORANDUM FOR:   District Commander, 5th District Office of Special
                     Investigations, Wright-Patterson Air Force Base,
                     Dayton, Ohio
   SUBJECT:          UNCONVENTIONAL AIRCRAFT




        1. On 5 October 1950, Mr. Mariana advised that the camera
   used by him in the taking of the moving picture film transmitted
   under separate cover, was a Revere, 16 mm. , magazine loading type
   with an F. 1. 9 wide-angle, telephoto lens. Mr. Mari ana stated
   that the settings used at the time were: shutter opening F. 22
   and focal setting., infinity. Film make and type was Eastman
   Kodachrome, daylight type, and speed at which pictures were taken
   was siXteen frames per second. Mr. Mariana .further advised that
   in operating the camera intaking the pictures., he had "panned"
   the camera slowly from right to left.
        2. A further check of the records of Base Operations, Great
   Falls Air Force Base, Great Falls, Montana, indicates that an
   F-94 type aircraft, #2503., and F- 94 type aircraft., #2502, landed
   at Great Falls Air Force Base at 1133 and 1130 hours (Mountain
   Standard Time) respectively on 15 August 1950. These aircraft
   were assigned the 449th Fighter Squadron, Ladd Air Force Base,
   Alaska. Mr. Mariana stated that almost immediately after taking
   the motion pictures, two USAF jet type aircrafthad flown across
   the sky east of him, heading in a southerly direction.

         keeps a record of the observable celestial phenomena or planets
         reports that there were no such phenomena observable on 15 August 1950.
    6. Distance o!objects from observer:


-

              d.ir~ction.

    7. Mr. Mariana states object was in sight to him a total of about
         twenty seconds and about seven seconds to his secretary, Miss Raunig.
    s. Mr. Mariana states that the appearance of the object was:
         a. Of a bright clean aluminwn surface.
         b. disc shape-apparentl:3' had depth
         c . Objects appeared to be of metal material
         d. According to observer, obj ects were about fi.fty feet in diameter

                  diameter a.bout three-quarters of the wing span of a C-54
                  when viewed from the s i de .

    11.

         No  evidence o.f exhaust.

         b. No aerodynamic list of fuselage observed.

                                       REI.ATIVE TO OBSERVER

         1.  Name of observer: Nicholas Mariana
         2.  Address : 2919 First Avenue North, Great Falls, Montana.
         3.  Occupation: Baseball club manager.
         4. Great Falls Baseball
         5. Pertinent hobbies: Observer is not an amateur astronomer,
             pilot or engineer; however, he has broadcast sporting events and
             is interested in photography
         6. Ability to determine color, speed and size of moving objects of
             observer should be relatively good in view of his experience as a
             sporting events announcer.
         7. Reliability of observer: Enjoys an excellent reputation in the
             local community and is regarded as a reliable, trustworthy and
             honest individual.
         a. Notes relative to observer on sightings in general: Mr. Mariana
         sta stated to the undersigned that he had read numerous newspaper
             articles on the subject of flying discs and flying saucers and had
             not attached very much significance to them. However, Mr. Mariana
             almost invariably carries a camera in order that he may make
             "opportunity pictures" and had previously thought that if he could
             see such unconventional aircraft in flight, he would take motion
             pictures of them.
             b. Observer' s attention was definitely drawn to objects by their
                 glint of light.
         9. Witnesses: Miss Virginia Raunig, 112 Second Avenue North, age 19,
             employed as a secretary. Miss Raunig impressed interrogator as
             being a fairly reliable individual and of good sound judgment. Miss
             Raunig continued sightings and number of objects and in substance
             verified Mr. Mari ana ' s statements as regards objects• appearance,
             shape, speed, sound and direction of flight.

            seventy-seven degrees, dew point, forty-five degrees, a ltimeter J0.02

NT 4- 28/83,10/177283                                            ll August 1952

From:      Chier Photographer Delbert c. Newhouse, 177283, U.S.N.
To:        Commanding Officer, Hill Air Force Base, Ogden, Utah

Subj :     Motion Pictures ot Unidentified Objects in Air

Encl:      (1)   One {1) fifty-foot roll of processed 16mm color motion
                 picture film

1. Enclosure (1) is forwarded herewith tor whatever value it may have in con­
nection with your investiption of the so- cal.led "Flying Saucers 11 • Pertinent
data follows:

Place:           About 7 miles north of Tremonton, Utah on U.S. Highway No. 30S.

Time:            lllO Wednesday 2 July 1952.
Camera.:         B&H Automaster, magazine load, 3" telephotolens, 16 f'rames per
                 second. Handheld.

Exposure :       First few feet - f : 8. l3ala.nce - f : 16. Clear sun, cl.oudless sky.
                 EKCo Kodachrome film - Da.ylight. Exposure was changed with the
                 mistaken idea of darkening the sky to gain greater contrast . The
                 result was merely general. under exposures.

General Background: Driving f'rom Washington, D.C. to Portland, Oregon, on
          the morning of 2 July my -wife noticed a group of objects in the
          sky that she could not identify. She asked me to stop the car and
          look. There was a group of about ten or twelve objects - that bore
          no relation to anything I had seen before - milling about in a
          rought formation and proceeding in a westerly direction. I opened
          the luggage compartment of the car and got my camera out of a suit­
          ease. Loa.ding it hurriedly, I exposed approximately thirty feet of
          film. There va.s no reference point in the sky and it was impossible
          for me to make a:ny estimate of speed, size, altitude or distance.
          Toward the end one of the objects reversed course and proceeded away
          fran the group. I held the camera still and al.loved this single
          one to cross the field o~ view, picking it up again and repeating
          for three or four such passes. By this time all of the objects
          had disappeared. I expended the balance of the f'ilm late that
          afternoon on a mountain somewhere in Idaho.




                                                 /s/
                                                Delbert C. Newhouse

                                                  October 29, 1952




    President HarI7 Truman
    White Rouse
    Wa shington, D. O.
    Dear Mr . . President:
         Permit me to take the liberty of writing 7ou on
    "flying saucer•"·
          It is becoming generally known that the Air Force
    has a report concluding that fiying saucers originate from
    sources outside this planet. Articles like that of Robert
    S. Allen, dated Sept. 26th are being photostated and circulated
    privately. The statements of Mr. DesVergers of West Palm
    :Beach, Florida, concerning his flying saucer experience and
    what he was told by the militacy and other incidents have
    convinced many including myself that the 11 saucers 11 are real
    and interplanetacy.
         I think it is unf"ortunate that a matter which is
    of such great import must be investigated privately by citizens
    who would like to and ahou1d be permitted to cooperate with
    and analyz6 with their government and military authorities.
    I think people who are afraid to tell the people of the
    possibilities of the flying saucers should bolster their
    faith in the people of this countcy and democracy.
         I hope you unveil the secrecy of the air force
    report. I also hope 7ou decide that this is not a matter of
    military secrecy and that events concerning flying saucer•
    be made availabl e to the public as they unfold.

                              Very truly yourt,



                              Robert J. Gorman

De~~ Misses Neuwirth and SchWlng:

    Your letter of 15 September 1952 was referred to us by the National
Security Council. As they told you ln their letter of 17 September 1952,
we are responsfole for the "flying saucer" tnvestigatton.

     Wlth regard to your questions about "flying saucer" reports, we
are incloslng a standard release which sets forth the offlclal Air Force
attitude and philosophy on the subject. We hope that tt will answer your
questions satisfactorily.

      The only report we have received of a "fire-breathing monster"
allegedly seen tn \Vest Vtrglnla has been through the press. Because
thi s observation was made at night and under conditions of cxcitement
and, furthermore, since the story has dted a natural death without
further mention in the press, we dtscount lt fdirely. It has been noted
in the past that a flurry of ''flying saucer" reports wlll stimulate the
imagination and gtve rise to many complementary reports evolved through
assoclation. We are convtnved that the report from West Vtrglnia
represents just such a case where the necessary stimulus was provided
by the meteor sighting rel)()rts of the preceding Friday night.

     We hope that these brtef comments have provided satisfactory answers
to your questlo~         '\. I"- _       -r

PROBI»I
    l. To prepare DF to ATIC and non-milltary letters to Prof. John C.
Weber, Mr. John M. Hollywood and Mr. G. Francis Nauhei.mer.

FACTS AND DISCUSSION
     2. In letters addressed to General Samford, tbe above-named gentl.emen
offer theories as to the cause of aerial. phencmesn which they believe ms:y
account for unidentified flying object sightings . It is noteworthy that
Prof. Weber is Associate Professor of El.ectrical Engineering at the U'niversity
of Wisconsin, Mr. Hol.lyvood is an engineer for the CBS Laboratories, and Mr.
Bauheimer is Director of the Glare Research Institute.
     3. Since the proposal.s of Prof. Weber and Mr. Hol.lyvood coincide
rather closely with tbe experiments of Mr. Scott of the Engineer Research
and Development Lab at Fort Belvoir as well as with the ideas of Dr. Possony,
their letters have been reviewed very caref'ully. Copies are being maintained
here for further study.
     4 . Original letters received from Prof . Weber and Mr. Hollywood will
be forwarded to ATIC by DF after signature by D/ I of replies.
     5. Replies to the three gentlemen are attached.        They have been written
in the first person from General Samford.
     6. Subject matter is not considered suitable for inclusion in the Daily
Staff Digest.
ACTION RECOMMENDED
     7. Bees.use of the professional status of the three correspondents,
it is strongly recommended that General Samford sign the attached replies.

                                                 15 September 1952

                       MEMORANDUM FOR RECORD

     PROBLEM :

        1. To prepare non- military letter to Mrs. Nellie T. Dull of Staunton,
     Virginia.

     FACTS AND DISCUSSION :

        2. On 1 Aug 52 Mr.s. Dull wrote in a letter to USAF Intelligence in
     which she theorized, in a completely amateurish manner, that electro­
     magnetism could account for 11flying saucers' . The re was absolutely no
     technical foundation to her ideas, and, actually, she presented the entire
•'   thing in the form of a question, viz. "please let me know if this is the
     answer."

        3. A routine reply, thanking her for the suggestion, was dispatched
     by this office 20 August 1952.

        4. In a second letter dated 12 Aug 52, Mrs. Dull expresses concern
     that her ideas were "stolen" by Mr. Noel Scott of the Engineer Research
     and Development Lab since the latter announced his ionization theory
     shortly after Mrs. Dull wrote her first letter.

         5. Copies of both letters from Mrs. Dull and our reply    are
     attached.

       6. A reply has been prepared, the contents of which should alleviate
     Mrs. Dull's concern.

       7. Subject matter is not considered suitable for inclusion in the
     Daily Staff Digest.

     ACTION RECOMMENDED:

       8. Approval, signature and dispatch of attached letter.

                                                      AFOIN-2A2
                                                      Maj Fournet/dbv/71016
                                                      13 September 1952

                               MEMORANDUM FOR RECORD

         PROBLEM :

           1. To prepare a non - military letter to Mr. E. L . Lull of Salt
         Lake City.

         FACTS AND DISCUSSION:

            2. In a telegram to this Directorate dated 29 July 1952 (AF IN
         14051), Mr. Lull states that, based on certain recent data, his
         calculations in his book "Daniel and the Atomic Equations of the
         Loaves" may be in error by about 2 years.

            3. A copy of this book was obtained through the Army Library
         from the Library of Congress. It is significant that the latter
         classifies the book as BS (Bible).

             4. This a nalyst must plead innocent to any accusation that he
         is a theologian. Nonetheless, on the assumption that any infor­
         mation regardless of origin or nature may possibly prove helpful
         to Intelligence and applying amateur theology, the book was
         s canned a nd the following conclusions derived:

                 a. Mr. Lull, who is an engineer, has attempted to
         establish a correlation between certain significant dates in history
         and specific passages in the Testaments and the Book of Mormon
         by the use of mathematical a nalyses.

                 b. Further, by the same analyses, he arrives at certain
         future dates which, by a nalogy, he concludes are indicative of
         extreme significan ce to the world. One such date is 4 Oct 54. It
         is implied that a revelation will be made on that date in fulfillment
         of one of Dan iel's prophecies in the ol d Testament an d that this
         revelation will be a joyous rather than a grievous one.

            5. It is assumed that Mr. Lull's statement to the effect that his
         calculations may be in error by about 2 years refers to the 4 Oct 54
         derivation and, further, that the error is positive, i.e. that the date

--- PAGE 202 ---
SECURITY INFORMATION




should be 4 Oct 52. This assumption is based on the urgency with
which Mr. Lull informed us of his error (by telegram).

   6. It is additionally assumed that Mr. Lull intends that the
aforementioned date be associated with Project Blue Book since:

            a. He states in his telegram that the basis for his belief
that he made an error in his original calculations (presumably re
4 Oct 54) is "the recent hyperspace demonstrations of the Disc Company
in free space" .

              b. His telegram was dispatched at the height of the most
recent 11flying saucer" flurry in the press (the date of the telegram is
the same as that on which General Samford held a press conference on
the subject) .
   7 . Accordingly, a reply to Mr. Lull has been prepared expressing
appreciation and stating that due notice has been taken of his con­
clusions.

   8. Additionally, this analyst has marked 4 Oct down as a red letter
day and is eagerly awaiting its arrival since it apparently means that
the mystery of the 11flying saucers 11 will finally be solved and his toil
towards that end will no longer be necessary.

  9. Subject matter is not considered suitable for inclusion in the
Daily Staff Digest.

ACTION RECOMMENDED:

  10. Approval and dispatch of attached letter to Mr. Lull.

COORDINATION:

     PROBLEM

          1.   To prepare an R & R t o rosM...OP.I .
     FACTS AND DISCUSSION

          2. A personal letter from Mr. William A. Berridge to General Vandenberg was
     referred toOSAP-OPI for preparation of a reply for signature in AJ'CCS. In this
     letter Mr. Berridge reports the sighting of an unidentified flying object and com­
     ments that he is disturbed over recent public remarks made by D/I personnel on this
     subject.

 fJ       3. Major Fournet discussed the letter with OSA.ll!OEI.. at the latter's request
     and indicated that AFOIN-2A2 would be happy to suggest certain appropriat• comments
     which could be incorporated into a reply. It was understood by OPI that AJ'OIN-2A2
     was not undertaking the complete reply since it was largely a matter requiring public
     relations treatment.

          4. The atta ched R & R sets forth those comments and suggestions considered
     appropriate from the D/I standpoint.

          5.   Subject matter is not considered suitable for inclusion in the Dally Staff
     Digest.

     ACTION RECOMMENDED
          6.   Approval, signature and dispatch of attached R & R.

                                        MEMORANDUM FOR RECORD


PROBLEM:

     1. To advise the Army and Navy as to the current Air Force intere st
in unidentified flying objects and gain their cooperati on in reporting
such data as may become availabl e to Army and Naval personnel .

FACTS AND DISCUSSION:

     2 . In the past, AFOIN has requested that the Army and :Navy assist the
Air Force in the collection of data pertaining to uni dentified flying objects .
Our desires were made known in a letter AFOIN, Subject : "Reporting of Infor­
mation on Unconventional Aircraft," 18 September 1950 which was signed by
Major General Cabell .

     3 . When the latest AF Instructions on this subject were issued on 29
April 1952 (AFL 200- 5) action to advise the Army and Navy of the existence
of new instructions was deferred at the request of ATIC pending the develop­
ment of an "Obs er ver s Questionnaire . " This form was to be used as an aid
in the extraction of information from observers . General use of this form
is not contemplated at this time .

     4 . At the working level, the Navy has asked to be informed as to the
current Air Force desires in this matter soonest . At levels lower than ¥A,s
headquarters echelons of the Air Force have mis - stated our desires to
echelons of the Navy. Instructions on this subject withl~ he Navy presently
are conflicting and confused.

      5 . Not for inclusion in the Daily Staff Digest .
RECO!IMENDATION:

     6 . That D/F to the Army and Hemo to the Navy be forwarded, asking
their cooperation in the reporting of unidentified flying objects and
outline a desirable reporting procedure in accordance with AFL 200-5 .
ACTION :

     7 . Recommended letters have been prepared.                    Request they be signed
and forwarded.
COORDINATION:

it occurred.. It is desired. to have Mr. Springer contacted. to determine
certain in.folllllB.tion bearing on Project Blue Book.

July 31, 1952



Major General John Sanf'ord.
War Department
Washington, D.C.
Sir:

     General Sanford everybody is taling about the flying saucers
I don't know how I ever got enough nerve to write you but I think it
is my turn now to p.1t in my nickels worth.
     As one serviceman to another I think I can talk frank with you.
I think I know the man who is responsible for the .f'ly:i..ng saucers, it
is a long story but I w.U1 try to make it as brief as possible.
     In 1929 at an inventor's meeting in Long Beach in the Rainbow Pier
I met a man who called himsel.f, at that time, Samuel P. Jones he had a
motor that everyone thought was a fake. He would explain it to you but
no one would believe him but me. So as a dumb cluck I tried to give
the man a helping hand, he said that this motor could be made in any
size or horsepower, could be put in ships, locomotives, cars and
airplanes. At that tlilme I knew a doctor in Long Beach who bad a
clinic and a yacht, I took this Jones to this do"btor thinking that this
doctor might finance him for a motor for his yacht. After the doctor talked
to the man awhile, the doctor didn't beieve him, thou~ he was nuts, so
then I forgot all about this man Jones until World War II. I met this
same man in the South Pacific and he was a Skipper in the Army Transport
Service. Then after seeing him again and knowing the position he held
I took it for granted that he must be pretty smart; not nuts. I became
very friendly with this man we chummed together for a coule of years
in the South Pacifica and every onee in awhile he 'WOUl.d tak about his
invention. I asked him if' this invention was so good why is it that the
Government wouldn't take it. This is what he told me, that he had tried
to get the Government to take it and they laughed at hmm and he finally
got an interview with President Roosevelt and the President told him that
at that time he also had an idea be.fore Congress that if it didn't work
out they would all go to the insane asylum. He also told him that his
idean was a thousand years ahead of time.
       Later 17\1947 I got a letter from this Jones asking me to come to him,
which I did, in H~vre, Montana he had a plane that I would call built with
this motor, this was the most damnable looking plane I have ever seen. It
was made like a saucer, about 17 feet in diameter and it would fly through
the air with a control made from the base of a telephone. Thia I saw with
my own eyes. He said that it would fly so fast through the air that the
air would melt, and I asked him "how fast will it go", he said actu.all.y he
didn't know but would figure it made a speed of 3 to 4000 miles per hour.

.,


                                  CO PY


     Major Gennal. John Sanford                         Juq 31, 1952
                                                           Page 2


          Again, I asked him why he didn't do something about it and he said
     it was impossible. I also, asked him why he didn't pa.tent this machine and
     he gave me amny reasons, taat each and every pa.rt about the machine had already
     been invented and it would take a millionaire to procure the patent rights
     to this invention. I also asked him wasn't he afraid that someone would
     steal it f'ran him and he told me that the public was funny that they couldn't
     see no further than the nose on their face and that if' you had a secret that
     you wanted to keep to tell everybcxiy and nobody would believe you.
          About three months later I received a let~er from this man telling me
     that he was ready to make a test, a few days later was the first time I
     heard about the f ' ~ saucers. Since that tJmne I have received many letters
     from this man Jones. About three weeks ago I received a letter from him again
     telling me that over Baltimore, Washington, Alexander Virginia, etc., that
     he was going to let the whole world know that his invention was possible.
     Then I went to the American Broadcastin~ Company, a couple of' newspapers
     and tried to tell them this story. They wouldn' t even listen to me and it
     has happened again. If this man Jones is not the inventor of the flying
     saucer how is it that he knows two or three weeks ahead of time when they are
     going to fly? I think that if you will .find this man Jones, I know you will
     get this .fantastic story straightened out. His address I do not lmow but he
     writes me to :my box in Detroit, Michigan and I receive a fetter maybe two
     or three weeks later. When I saw the plane or whatever you want to call it,
     in the air, it would go so :fast you would hardly see it. It was round like
     a saucer, had a tail like a plane, and when it would sway (as I would say)
     in the air sometimes it would look like a pencil., sometimes like a egg, and
     again it would look like a balloon so you take it from here. Use your pm
     judgment, you have my permission to do anything you want with this letter.
     My name is H. D. Springer, I have no permammti address but I live in Detroit,
     I am going back east tonight at 9 o'clock P.R.
                                                       Sincere~ yours~


                                                       H. D. SPRINGER
